The '''2026 United States House of Representatives elections''' are scheduled to be held on Tuesday, November 3, 2026, as part of the [[2026 United States elections|2026 midterm elections]] during President [[Donald Trump]]'s [[Second presidency of Donald Trump|second nonconsecutive term]]. Voters will elect representatives from all 435 [[List of United States congressional districts|congressional districts]] across each of the fifty [[U.S. state]]s, and five of the six [[Non-voting members of the United States House of Representatives|non-voting delegates]] from the [[Washington, D.C.|District of Columbia]] and the inhabited [[Insular area|U.S. territories]].

Special elections are also being held on dates throughout 2026. Numerous other federal, state, and local elections, including [[2026 United States Senate elections|elections to the Senate]], will be held on this date. The winners of this election will serve in the [[120th United States Congress]], with seats apportioned among the states based on the [[2020 United States census]] and revised district maps as a result of the [[2025–2026 United States redistricting]].

Primary elections for all 435 House seats, and five of the six non-voting delegate seats were held starting on March 3, and concluded on September 15, 2026.

==Retirements==
===Summary===
<!--[[file:2026 United States House of Representatives elections retirements or losses of renomination map.svg|thumb|350px|Retiring incumbents by district<br />
{{legend|#92C5DE|Democratic incumbent}} {{legend|#0671B0|Democratic incumbent retiring or lost renomination}}{{legend|#F48882|Republican incumbent}} {{legend|#CA0120|Republican incumbent retiring, lost renomination, or withdrew}}
{{legend|#999999|Independent incumbent}}{{legend|#6D6D6D|No incumbent}}{{efn |As a result of redistricting.}} {{legend|#808080|Vacant}}{{efn |As a result of death, resignation, or expulsion.}}]]-->
<!--Eleanor Holmes Norton is NOT a representative and should not be counted as such. That is why the sentence is "(total retirements minus 1) representatives and one non-voting delegate".-->
60 representatives and two non-voting delegates (26 Democrats and 36 Republicans) have announced their retirement, 29 of whom (9 Democrats and 20 Republicans) are retiring to run for other offices. This is the second-most retirements of representatives in a single election cycle in US history next to [[1992 United States House of Representatives elections|1992]], which saw 65 total retirements.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Santaliz |first1=Kate |last2=Nichols |first2=Hans |title=House retirements tied for most this century |url=https://www.axios.com/2026/03/02/members-of-congress-retirements-record-high |date=March 2, 2026 |access-date=March 9, 2026 |website=[[Axios (website)|Axios]] |url-status=live |url-access=registration |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260303165503/https://www.axios.com/2026/03/02/members-of-congress-retirements-record-high |archive-date=March 3, 2026}}</ref>

==Resignations==
Two seats will be left vacant on the day of the general election until the next Congress due to resignations.

===Democratic===
#{{ushr|FL|20|X}}: [[Sheila Cherfilus-McCormick]] resigned on April 21, 2026, following money laundering allegations.<ref name="SCM">{{cite web |last1=Gorman |first1=Reese |last2=Goba |first2=Kadia |last3=González |first3=Oriana |title=Democratic Rep. Sheila Cherfilus-McCormick Resigns |url=https://www.notus.org/congress/sheila-cherfilus-mccormick-resigns |access-date=April 21, 2026 |work=[[NOTUS (website)|NOTUS]] |date=April 21, 2026}}</ref>

===Republican===
#{{ushr|TX|23|X}}: [[Tony Gonzales]] resigned on April 14, 2026, after admitting to an affair.<ref name="Gonzales">{{cite web |date=2026-04-13 |title=Embattled GOP Rep. Tony Gonzales announces he's stepping down from Congress |url=https://www.cnn.com/2026/04/13/politics/tony-gonzales-leaving-congress |publisher=CNN |language=en}}</ref>

==Crossover seats==
This is a list of congressional seats that voted for one party in the [[2024 United States presidential election|2024 presidential election]] and another in the [[2024 United States House of Representatives elections|2024 House elections]].<ref>{{cite web |last=Kondik |first=Kyle |title=The 2024 Crossover House Seats: Overall Number Remains Low with Few Harris-District Republicans |url=https://centerforpolitics.org/crystalball/the-2024-crossover-house-seats-overall-number-remains-low-with-few-harris-district-republicans/ |date=January 16, 2025 |access-date=January 18, 2025 |language=en-US}}</ref>

===Democratic===
There were originally 13 districts which [[Donald Trump]] won in 2024 that are represented by Democrats. This table only includes the 11 remaining after mid-decade redistricting. Four of these districts were already won by Trump but were made more Republican-leaning through redistricting.<ref name=":3">{{cite web |last1=Bowman |first1=Bridget |title=Trump's victory sets up fight for the House on his turf in 2026 |url=https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/congress/trumps-victory-sets-fight-house-turf-2026-rcna184017 |publisher=[[NBC News]] |access-date=16 March 2025 |date=14 December 2024}}</ref>

==Mid-decade redistricting changes==
{{main|2025–2026 United States redistricting}}

[[File:2026 United States mid-decade redistricting expected partisan gains.svg|thumb|349x349px|States that have undergone mid-decade redistricting prior to the 2026 elections colored by which party is expected to benefit.{{col-start}}{{col-2}}{{legend|#0671B0|Finalized new map; Democratic gain expected}}{{legend|#808080 |Voluntary redistricting rejected}}{{col-2}}{{legend|#C90000|Finalized new map; Republican gain expected}}{{legend|#b0b0b0|Redistricting challenge rejected}}{{legend|#000000|New map finalized; later struck down in court}}{{col-end}}]]
[[File:US Congress mid-decade redistricting causes 2026.svg|thumb|349x349px|States that have undergone mid-decade redistricting prior to the 2026 elections colored by the reason for redistricting.{{col-start}}{{col-2}}{{legend|#F0F000|Voluntary redistricting enacted}}{{legend|#808080|Voluntary redistricting rejected}}{{col-2}}{{legend|#C90000|Court-ordered/mandatory redistricting}}{{legend|#b0b0b0|Redistricting challenge rejected}}
{{legend|#000000|New map finalized; later struck down in court}}{{col-end}}]]
In the United States, all states with multiple congressional districts are required to revise their district maps following each decennial census to account for population changes. In 2026, most states will use the same districts created in the [[2020 United States redistricting cycle|redistricting cycle following the 2020 census]], which were first used in the 2022 elections. Maps have changed or would change in several states, often due to legal challenges made based on partisan or racial [[Gerrymandering in the United States|gerrymandering]].{{Table alignment}}

As of {{monthyear}}, several states have seen challenges to their [[List of United States congressional districts|congressional district]] maps that were put in place during the [[2020 United States redistricting cycle|redistricting cycle]] brought upon by the results of the 2020 census. In [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Arkansas|Arkansas]], a federal court dismissed a case against their congressional map that argued the map did not comply with the [[Voting Rights Act]].<ref>{{cite web |last=Grajeda |first=Antoinette |date=2025-06-06 |title=Federal panel rules in favor of state in Arkansas congressional redistricting lawsuit |url=https://arkansasadvocate.com/2025/06/06/federal-panel-rules-in-favor-of-state-in-arkansas-congressional-redistricting-lawsuit/ |access-date=2025-07-23 |website=Arkansas Advocate |language=en-US}}</ref> The [[Supreme Court of Florida|Florida Supreme Court]] dismissed a challenge regarding the Florida's congressional map, finding a new plurality-Black congressional district would be against federal law.<ref>{{cite web |title=In boon for House GOP, Florida Supreme Court sides with DeSantis, upholds congressional map |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2025/07/17/florida-supreme-court-sides-with-desantis-upholds-congressional-map-00459893 |access-date=2025-07-23 |website=Politico |date=July 17, 2025}}</ref>

In [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Georgia|Georgia]], litigation is still ongoing regarding appeals to the newly drawn congressional map that was used in the 2024 elections. However, it was not resolved before the filing deadline for the 2026 Congressional elections.<ref>{{cite web |last=Cook |first=Brianna |date=2025-01-22 |title=Georgia's redistricting maps return to court, challenging voting rights protections |url=https://wgxa.tv/news/state-news/georgias-redistricting-maps-return-to-court-challenging-voting-rights-protections-gerrymandering-legislative-congressional-brad-raffensperger |access-date=2025-07-23 |publisher=WGXA |language=en}}</ref> In [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Louisiana|Louisiana]], litigation remained unresolved.<ref name="SC">{{cite web |last=Howe |first=Amy |date=2025-06-27 |title=Supreme Court punts decision on Louisiana's congressional map to next term |url=https://www.scotusblog.com/2025/06/supreme-court-punts-decision-on-louisianas-congressional-map-to-next-term/ |access-date=2025-07-23 |website=SCOTUSblog |language=en-US}}</ref> In South Carolina, the [[South Carolina Supreme Court]] ruled that partisan gerrymandering is not in violation of the state's constitution, keeping the maps in place.<ref name=":0">{{cite web |last=George |first=Dustin |date=September 17, 2025 |title=State Supreme Court rules partisan gerrymandering constitutional |url=https://www.wspa.com/news/state-news/state-supreme-court-rules-partisan-gerrymandering-constitutional/ |access-date=September 17, 2025 |publisher=[[WSPA-TV]] |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251017092625/https://www.wspa.com/news/state-news/state-supreme-court-rules-partisan-gerrymandering-constitutional/ |archive-date=October 17, 2025 |url-status=dead}}</ref> In [[Texas]], a trial was held in federal court in [[El Paso, Texas|El Paso]] to determine whether its congressional map discriminated against Black and Latino voters in violation of the VRA,<ref name=TXmap1>{{cite web |last=Kamal |first=Sameea |date=May 21, 2025 |title=Federal trial opens to determine whether Texas discriminated in redrawn redistricting maps |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2025/05/21/texas-redistricting-map-court-challenge-discrimination/ |access-date=August 21, 2025 |work=[[The Texas Tribune]]}}</ref> but the case was paused pending expected new maps in the state.<ref name="LULAC">{{cite web |date=August 20, 2025 |title=League of United Latin American Citizens v. Abbott |url=https://thearp.org/litigation/lulac-v-abbott/ |access-date=August 21, 2025 |website=The American Redistricting Project}}</ref>

In [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Utah|Utah]], a partisan gerrymander case resulted in a map which creates a safe Democratic district comprising the majority of [[Salt Lake County]] following a district court ruling.<ref name=":4">{{cite web |last=Aerts |first=Lindsay |date=November 11, 2025 |title=Redistricting ruling: Judge rules against Utah legislature and picks plaintiffs map 1 for Utah's 2026 midterms |url=https://www.abc4.com/news/politics/inside-utah-politics/redistricting-ruling-judge-rules-against-utah-legislature-and-picks-plaintiffs-map-1-for-utahs-2026-midterms/ |access-date=November 11, 2025 |publisher=[[KTVX]]}}</ref> In [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Wisconsin|Wisconsin]], a challenge to the state's current congressional map, arguing it was an unlawful partisan gerrymander, was dismissed by the [[Wisconsin Supreme Court]].<ref name="autogenerated3">{{cite web |date=2025-06-25 |title=Wisconsin Supreme Court refuses to hear challenges to the state's congressional district boundaries |url=https://apnews.com/article/wisconsin-redistricting-congress-supreme-court-65f1b019febe9ca0d0db234b975b3445 |access-date=2025-07-23 |publisher=Associated Press |language=en}}</ref> A new map was required in [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Ohio|Ohio]] due to the Congressional Redistricting Procedures Amendment to Section XI of the [[Constitution of Ohio|state's constitution]], resulting in two districts becoming more Republican leaning.<ref name="OHnewmap"/>
===Out-of-cycle partisan redistricting efforts===
[[Greg Abbott|Governor Greg Abbott]] called a [[special session]] of the [[Texas Legislature]] to redraw its congressional districts, leading to the Texas Legislature passing new maps resulting in 5 new Republican-leaning districts.<ref name="tg">{{cite web |last=Yang |first=Maya |date=August 29, 2025 |title=Texas governor signs new redistricting bill, setting up loss of Democratic seats in US House |url=https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2025/aug/29/texas-governor-greg-abbott-signs-redistricting-bill |access-date=August 29, 2025 |work=[[The Guardian]]}}</ref> On November 18, a panel of the [[United States District Court for the Western District of Texas|District Court for the Western District of Texas]] issued a preliminary injunction blocking the new map, putting the former map back in place,<ref name=":5">{{cite web |last1=Schouten |first1=Fredreka |last2=Cohen |first2=Ethan |date=November 18, 2025 |title=Federal court bars Texas from using new Republican-friendly US House map in midterms |url=https://www.cnn.com/2025/11/18/politics/texas-redistricting-trump-court-ruling |access-date=November 18, 2025 |publisher=[[CNN]]}}</ref> but the Supreme Court later placed an administrative stay on the ruling, allowing the new maps to stand.<ref name=TXmap>{{cite web |last=Sherman|first=Mark|date=December 4, 2025|title=Supreme Court allows Texas to use a congressional map favorable to Republicans in 2026|publisher=Associated Press|url=https://apnews.com/article/supreme-court-redistricting-texas-trump-02b07b477b153f23ed5c387f2f9ae0c4|access-date=February 15, 2026}}</ref> [[Ron DeSantis|Governor Ron DeSantis]] has expressed interest in redrawing [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Florida|Florida's]] congressional map, and the Florida House has created a special redistricting committee.<ref>{{cite web |last=Rohrer |first=Gray |title=Florida Gov. DeSantis says he's ready for redistricting – with or without new census |url=https://www.tallahassee.com/story/news/local/state/2025/08/11/desantis-uthmeier-redistricting-census/85608955007/ |access-date=2025-08-11 |website=Tallahassee Democrat |language=en-US}}</ref> [[Mike Kehoe|Governor Mike Kehoe]] of [[Missouri]] called a special session on August 29, 2025, which redrew the solid Democratic [[Missouri's 5th congressional district|5th district]] to a solid Republican district, allowing the GOP to pick up a seat.<ref name=":1">{{cite web |last=Hines |first=Lani |date=2025-09-28 |title=Gov. Mike Kehoe signs gerrymandered congressional map into law |url=https://www.komu.com/news/state/gov-mike-kehoe-signs-gerrymandered-congressional-map-into-law/article_fdc64bd2-0dac-4daf-8000-ec599620b4f8.html |access-date=2025-09-28 |publisher=KOMU 8 |language=en}}</ref> However, on September 3, the [[Missouri Supreme Court]] unanimously ruled that the referendum on the new maps was sufficient and must be put on the ballot, thus leaving the 2022 maps in place for the 2026 elections.<ref name=":6">{{Cite news |last=Keller |first=Rudi |date=September 3, 2026 |title=Missouri Supreme Court blocks gerrymandered congressional map, orders referendum vote |url=https://missouriindependent.com/2026/09/03/missouri-supreme-court-blocks-gerrymandered-congressional-map-orders-referendum-vote/ |access-date=September 3, 2026 |work=Missouri Independent}}</ref> Republican state legislative leaders in [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in North Carolina|North Carolina]] announced a redraw of the state's congressional districts to make the 1st congressional district more Republican leaning.<ref name=NCmap/>

Talks and efforts about redistricting occurred in [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Nebraska|Nebraska]], [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in New Hampshire|New Hampshire]], [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Indiana|Indiana]], and [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Kansas|Kansas]], but either failed or did not gain traction.<ref>{{cite web |last=Pellish |first=Aaron |date=2025-08-08 |title=Kelly Ayotte rules out redistricting for New Hampshire |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2025/08/08/kelly-ayotte-rules-out-redistricting-for-new-hampshire-00501207 |access-date=2025-08-11 |website=[[Politico]]}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=II |first=Juan Salinas |date=2025-08-06 |title=Nebraska might join national 'wildfire' of mid-decade redistricting • Nebraska Examiner |url=https://nebraskaexaminer.com/2025/08/06/nebraska-might-join-national-wildfire-of-mid-decade-redistricting/ |access-date=2025-08-11 |website=[[Nebraska Examiner]] |language=en-US}}</ref><ref name=INmap/><ref name=KSmap/>

Democrats have threatened to retaliate against attempts to mid-decade redistrict for partisan gain and have stated the possibility of redrawing the congressional maps in blue states. In [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in California|California]], the [[California State Legislature|state legislature]] drafted a map eliminating five Republican districts to combat Texas's plan, which was approved by voters. In [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Virginia|Virginia]], members of the [[Virginia General Assembly|General Assembly]] announced a plan on October 23, 2025 to begin the process to redraw the state's congressional districts before the 2026 elections.<ref name="auto">{{cite web |date=2025-10-23 |title=Virginia Democrats Plan to Redraw House Maps in Redistricting Push |work=The New York Times |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2025/10/23/us/politics/virginia-democrats-redistrict.html |access-date=2025-10-23 |language=en}}</ref>

Democratic governor [[Wes Moore]] of [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Maryland|Maryland]] unsuccessfully pursued redistricting in his state.<ref name=MDmap/> Redistricting has been considered in [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Colorado|Colorado]], [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in New Jersey|New Jersey]], and [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections in New York|New York]]. Legal and political challenges make redistricting before 2026 highly unlikely.<ref>{{cite web |last=Toomer |first=Lindsey |date=2025-12-09 |title=In shift, Sen. Bennet supports Colorado constitutional amendment for emergency redistricting |url=https://coloradonewsline.com/briefs/bennet-supports-colorado-redistricting |access-date=2025-12-16 |website=Colorado Newsline |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |date=2025-07-22 |title=New Jersey governor doesn't rule out redistricting that could target GOP |url=https://www.newsweek.com/phil-murphy-does-not-rule-out-new-jersey-redistricting-2102531 |access-date=2025-08-09 |website=Newsweek |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |date=2025-08-04 |title=Hochul embraces gerrymandering in New York |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2025/08/04/hochul-embraces-gerrymandering-in-new-york-00492008 |access-date=2025-08-09 |website=Politico}}</ref>
{{Clear}}

![[#Alabama|Alabama]]
|data-sort-value=0 {{ya|text=New districts enacted}}
|In April 2026, the Supreme Court ruled on ''[[Louisiana v. Callais]]'' that stricter scrutiny had to be applied with regard to section 2 of the [[Voting Rights Act]]. Governor [[Kay Ivey]] was initially reluctant to call a special session after ''[[Allen v. Milligan]]'', but was lobbied to do so by the state attorney general and secretary of state, both of whom also filed emergency injunctions to lift the requirements levied on them by the federal courts.<ref>{{cite web |title=Gov. Ivey calls special session on Alabama redistricting |url=https://www.wbrc.com/2026/05/01/alabama-governor-call-special-session-redistricting/ |publisher=WBRC |date=May 2026 }}</ref> {{pb}}The Supreme Court voted 6–3 to lift the stay, paving the way for state legislators to redraw.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Schonfeld |first1=Zach |title=Supreme Court lifts block on Alabama Republicans' map |url=https://thehill.com/regulation/court-battles/5873190-supreme-court-alabama-redistricting/ |work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] |access-date=May 12, 2026 |date=May 11, 2026}}</ref> On May 26, 2026, an Alabama court blocked the new map, arguing that the ''[[Louisiana v. Callais]]'' decision does not change the fact that the map intentionally discriminates against African-American voters in violation of the Constitution.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Schonfeld|first1=Zach|title=Court blocks Alabama Republicans' congressional map|url=https://thehill.com/regulation/court-battles/5894918-alabama-republican-congressional-map-blocked/|work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] |access-date=May 26, 2026 |date=May 26, 2026}}</ref> On June 2, 2026, the [[Supreme Court of the United States|Supreme Court]] granted stay on the block, allowing the new map to be implemented.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Schonfeld|first1=Zach|last2=Vakil|first2=Caroline|title=Supreme Court clears way for Alabama Republicans to use congressional map for midterms|url=https://thehill.com/regulation/court-battles/5907001-supreme-court-alabama-redistricting/|work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] |access-date=June 3, 2026 |date=June 2, 2026}}</ref>

|In September 2023, a state circuit judge ruled that the map passed by the state legislature discriminated against Black voters, by redrawing the formerly plurality-Black 5th district to no longer have a Black plurality. Florida appealed this ruling and the map was reinstated by an appeals court.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Crowder |first1=Valerie |title=Protections for minority voters are at the center of a Florida redistricting case |url=https://www.npr.org/2024/09/11/nx-s1-5106385/florida-redistricting-case-fair-districts |publisher=[[NPR]] |access-date=23 December 2024 |date=12 September 2024}}</ref><wbr/> In July 2025, the Florida Supreme Court ruled 5–1 to keep the current maps in place, finding that creating a plurality-Black congressional district would violate the federal [[Equal Protection Clause]].<ref>{{cite web |last1=Rohrer |first1=Gray |title=Florida Supreme Court upholds DeSantis-backed congressional district maps |url=https://www.tallahassee.com/story/news/local/state/2025/07/17/florida-supreme-court-redistricting/85256877007/ |access-date=July 17, 2025 |work=[[Tallahassee Democrat]] |date=July 17, 2025}}</ref>{{pb}}On January 9, 2026, Governor [[Ron DeSantis]] called for a special session in April redrawing the state's congressional maps with the target of gaining 3 to 5 new Republican seats. However, Florida state law prevents deliberately redrawing congressional maps purely for partisan gain.<ref>{{cite web |title=DeSantis calls April special legislative session on Florida congressional redistricting|url=https://www.politico.com/news/2026/01/07/desantis-florida-redistricting-special-session-00713882|website=Politico|date=2026-01-07|access-date=2026-01-31|language=en}}</ref> On February 27, 2026, the [[Supreme Court of Florida]] rejected a petition to block the special session.<ref>{{cite web |title=Florida Supreme Court rejects bid to block DeSantis' redistricting special session, clears way for new congressional maps |url=https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/florida-supreme-court-rejects-bid-to-block-desantis-redistricting-special-session-clears-way-for-new-congressional-maps/ |publisher=[[CBS News]] |access-date=3 March 2026 |date=1 March 2026}}</ref> On April 29, 2026, the maps were approved by the [[Florida Legislature]].<ref>{{cite web |title=Florida legislature approves new congressional map intended to boost Republicans in midterms |url=https://apnews.com/article/florida-ron-desantis-donald-trump-redistricting-13e14f95a8d2b6afbc7e3e698f5f9256 |publisher=Associated Press |date=2026-04-29 |access-date=2026-04-29 |language=en}}</ref> On May 4, 2026, Governor DeSantis signed the new map into law.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Vakil |first1=Caroline |title=DeSantis signs new GOP-favored House map into law |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5855827-florida-redistricting-desantis-law/ |work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] |access-date=May 5, 2026 |date=May 4, 2026}}</ref>

2026 |work=Missouri Independent}}</ref> However, on September 3, the [[Missouri Supreme Court]] unanimously ruled that the referendum is sufficient and must be put on the ballot, thus leaving the 2022 |data-sort-value=0 {{ya|text=New districts enacted}}
|Ohio voters amended the constitution in 2018, creating a process that requires both Republicans and Democrats in the state legislature to vote on the new congressional map. |In ''League of United Latin American Citizens v. Until the Trump DOJ disagreed. |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2025/07/11/texas-redistricting-racial-gerrymandering-coalition-districts-trump/ |access-date=August 21, 2025 |work=[[The Texas Tribune]]}}</ref>{{pb}}{{pb}}In early 2025, the Trump Administration urged Texas officials to redraw congressional districts. This was followed by a meeting of the Texas Republican congressional delegation to discuss mid-decade redistricting to secure extra Republican seats.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.texastribune.org/2025/06/09/texas-redistricting-congress-house-republicans-midterms/ |title=Texas' GOP congressional delegation meets to discuss redrawing districts ahead of midterms |date=June 9, 2025 |access-date=July 16, 2025 |work=Texas Tribune |author=Dahlkamp, Owen}}</ref> On July 9, Governor [[Greg Abbott]] called a special session of the legislature to be held later that month with the goal of creating 5 new Republican seats. After Texas House Democrats staged a weeks-long walkout, the legislature passed the new maps on August 23, and Abbott signed them into law on August 29.<ref name="tg"/>{{pb}} The previous case was paused, pending new maps.<ref name="LULAC"/> After passage, on August 27, the court scheduled a preliminary injunction hearing to begin on October 1 to October 10.<ref name="oh">{{cite web |last=Jeffers |first=Gromer Jr. |date=August 27, 2025 |title=October hearing set on legality of new Texas congressional boundaries |url=https://www.union-bulletin.com/news/national/october-hearing-set-on-legality-of-new-texas-congressional-boundaries/article_ab089f7a-8380-5846-8a1d-fcf9921fd455.html |url-access=subscription |access-date=August 29, 2025 |work=Union-Bulletin}}</ref> On November 18, the panel issued a preliminary injunction blocking the new map, putting the former map back in place. The case on the 2021 maps remains open, pending action from the U.S. Supreme Court in ''[[Louisiana v. Callais]]''.<ref name=":5"/> On November 21, the U.S. Supreme Court issued an administrative stay on the lower court ruling, temporarily leaving the new map in place.<ref name=TXmap/> On December 4, the Supreme Court ruled 6–3 in allowing the map to stand for the 2026 elections.<ref>{{cite web |last=Howe |first=Amy |date=2025-12-04 |title=Supreme Court allows Texas to use redistricting map challenged as racially discriminatory |url=https://www.scotusblog.com/2025/12/supreme-court-allows-texas-to-use-redistricting-map-challenged-as-racially-discriminatory/ |access-date=2025-12-05 |website=SCOTUSblog |language=en-US}}</ref>
|data-sort-value=1 |{{decrease}} 3
|data-sort-value=0 |{{decrease}} 2
|data-sort-value=−1 |{{increase}} 5

![[#Utah|Utah]]
|data-sort-value=0 {{ya|text=New districts enacted}}
|In July 2023, in ''[[League of Women Voters v. Utah State Legislature]]'', the [[Utah Supreme Court]] heard arguments alleging that the Utah Legislature violated [[2018 Utah Proposition 4|a citizen-passed anti-gerrymandering proposition]] by dividing Salt Lake County voters into all four of Utah's districts. The case was sent back to a state district court, which held a hearing in January 2025. A ruling for the League of Women Voters was issued on August 25. New maps were expected to be drawn up by the Legislature by September 24 and finalized by November.<ref name=redrawn>{{cite web |last1=Schoenbaum |first1=Hannah |title=Judge rules Utah's congressional map must be redrawn for the 2026 elections |url=https://apnews.com/article/utah-redistricting-congressional-map-gerrymandering-a6722505b8e76eda5c73fc346eadd9aa |access-date=August 25, 2025 |publisher=[[Associated Press]] |date=August 25, 2025 |language=en}}</ref>
{{pb}}On October 6, the Utah Legislature passed a new map, which changed 2 Republican districts to be more competitive.<ref name=":2">{{cite web |last=Timm |first=Jane C. |date=October 6, 2025 |title=Utah lawmakers pass new GOP-backed congressional map |url=https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/elections/utah-lawmakers-pass-new-gop-backed-congressional-map-rcna235440 |access-date=November 11, 2025 |publisher=[[NBC News]]}}</ref> On November 10, Utah Third District Judge Dianna Gibson ruled that the new map also violated Proposition 4, opting to approve a plaintiff-submitted alternative, Map 1, which creates a safe Democratic district comprising only [[Salt Lake County]].<ref name=":4"/>
|{{increase}} 1
|{{steady}}
|{{decrease}} 1

![[#Virginia|Virginia]]
|{{no2|Districts left in place}}
|Democratic state lawmakers announced a plan to alter the state constitution to draw a new congressional map before the 2026 elections making 3-4 more Democratic seats.<ref name="auto"/>
{{pb}}The proposed mechanism was an amendment to the [[Constitution of Virginia]] allowing the [[Virginia General Assembly|General Assembly]] to modify the state's congressional districts if any other state did so between January 1, 2025 and October 31, 2030 for any reason other than normal decennial redistricting or to satisfy a court order. Redistricting was transferred from the General Assembly to a bipartisan commission in [[2020 Virginia Question 1|2020]], and the power would revert to the commission for the normal 2030 cycle. The amendment proposal first passed the General Assembly on October 31, 2025. The amendment procedure requires any proposal to pass in both houses of the General Assembly, then it must pass both houses again after the next [[Virginia House of Delegates]] election is held (every two years), then it must be approved in a referendum.<ref>{{cite news |last1=Khalil |first1=Jahd |title=Virginia Senate votes to propose redistricting amendment - VPM |url=https://www.vpm.org/general-assembly/2025-10-31/ga-hj6007-gerrymandering-senate-favola-stanley-vanvalkenburg-obenshain/ |publisher=VPM |date=31 October 2025}}</ref>  

[[Virginia Senate]] minority leader [[Ryan McDougle]], a Republican, sued Democratic House Speaker [[Don Scott (Virginia politician)|Don Scott]] over the legality of the amendment. On January 27, 2026, [[Tazewell County, Virginia|Tazewell County]] Circuit Court Judge Jack Hurley, Jr. ruled that the amendment could not satisfy the intervening-election requirement since early voting in the [[2025 Virginia elections]] was underway at the time of passage.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2026/jan/27/judge-strikes-virginia-democrats-congressional-redistricting|title=Judge strikes down Virginia Democrats' plan to redraw congressional districts|website=[[The Guardian]]|date=January 27, 2026}}</ref> On January 28, the [[Democratic Party of Virginia]] appealed the ruling; the court of appeals, citing the importance of the case, forwarded it to the [[Supreme Court of Virginia]] for an expedited ruling on February 4.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Vakil |first1=Caroline |title=Virginia Democrats appeal ruling on redistricting amendment |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5711198-redistricting-amendment-virginia-appealed/ |work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] |access-date=1 February 2026 |date=28 January 2026}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |title=Virginia Supreme Court expected hear redistricting challenge |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2026/02/04/virginia-supreme-court-will-hear-redistricting-challenge-00765490 |work=POLITICO |date=4 February 2026 |language=en}}</ref> On February 10, the General Assembly passed the amendment proposal the required second time and included a draft map that would likely net Democrats four seats.<ref>{{cite news |last1=Schneider |first1=Gregory |title=Virginia Democrats quickly send proposed redistricting map to Spanberger |url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/dc-md-va/2026/02/10/virginia-redistricting-spanberger-trump-map/ |newspaper=[[The Washington Post]] |access-date=11 February 2026 |archive-url=https://ghostarchive.org/archive/k6bWD |archive-date=10 February 2026 |date=10 February 2026 |url-status=live |url-access=subscription}}</ref> On February 13, the Supreme Court allowed the April 21 [[2026 Virginia redistricting amendment|referendum]] to go forward while it considered the matter. The referendum passed with 51.7% voting yes.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Mueller |first1=Julia |title=Democrats boost midterm edge with Virginia redistricting win: 5 takeaways |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5842396-virginia-redistricting-democrats-win-takeaways/ |work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] |access-date=April 22, 2026 |date=April 21, 2026}}</ref>  The next day, Judge Hurley ruled the referendum to be unconstitutional on the basis of another lawsuit and enjoined the state board of elections from certifying the result. Attorney General [[Jay Jones]] appealed the decision; the Supreme Court declined to stay it while the original case was ongoing and as a result the board was unable to certify the result at its May meeting.<ref>{{cite web|url=https://cardinalnews.org/2026/04/29/virginia-supreme-court-blocks-certification-of-redistricting-election-what-this-might-mean/|title=Virginia Supreme Court blocks certification of redistricting election. What this might mean.|website=Cardinal News|author=Dwayne Yancey|date=April 29, 2026}}</ref> <ref>{{cite web |last1=Schemmel |first1=Alec |title=Virginia court declares state's redistricting vote was unconstitutional in legal win for Republicans |url=https://www.foxnews.com/politics/virginia-court-declares-states-redistricting-vote-unconstitutional-legal-win-republicans |publisher=[[Fox News]] |access-date=April 27, 2026 |date=April 22, 2026}}</ref> The Supreme Court heard oral arguments in the case on April 27.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Vakil |first1=Caroline |title=Virginia Supreme Court mulls legality of Democratic redistricting referendum |url=https://thehill.com/regulation/court-battles/5851256-virginia-supreme-court-redistricting-case/ |work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] |access-date=April 27, 2026 |date=April 27, 2026}}</ref> 

On May 8, the Supreme Court invalidated the amendment in a 4–3 decision, with the majority agreeing with Judge Hurley's ruling that its first passage was too late to satisfy the intervening-election requirement.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Schonfeld |first1=Zach |last2=Mueller |first2=Julia |title=Virginia Supreme Court throws out redistricting results in blow to Democrats |url=https://thehill.com/regulation/court-battles/5869616-virginia-supreme-court-tosses-redistricting-map-2026-midterms/ |work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] |access-date=May 8, 2026 |date=May 8, 2026}}</ref>{{pb}}Following the ruling, Speaker Scott and Attorney General Jones filed an emergency appeal to the [[United States Supreme Court]].<ref>{{cite web |last=Wilder |first=Drew |date=2026-05-08 |title=Virginia Democrats to appeal ruling against redistricting to U.S. Supreme Court |url=https://www.nbcwashington.com/news/local/virginia-democrats-appeal-ruling-redistricting-us-supreme-court/4102136/?amp=1 |access-date=2026-05-08 |publisher=NBC Washington |language=en}}</ref> On May 15, the Supreme Court denied the appeal.<ref>{{cite web |last=Sherman |first=Mark |date=2026-05-15 |title=Supreme Court rejects Virginia's bid to restore congressional map favoring Democrats |url=https://apnews.com/article/supreme-court-virginia-redistricting-democrats-republicans-fdd33b89ac29cf2806f790f9b22ee2e9 |access-date=2026-05-15 |publisher=Associated Press |language=en}}</ref>

![[#Wisconsin|Wisconsin]]
|{{no2|Districts left in place}}
|In July 2025, a lawsuit was filed by two liberal law firms in a Wisconsin district court, asking to consider the constitutionality of Wisconsin's congressional maps.<ref>{{cite web |date=2025-07-22 |title=New Lawsuit Alleges Wisconsin's Congressional Map Violates State Constitution |url=https://www.democracydocket.com/news-alerts/wisconsin-congressional-map-discriminates-democrats-unconstitutional-lawsuit/ |access-date=2025-10-01 |website=Democracy Docket |language=en-US}}</ref> In September 2025, the Wisconsin Supreme court addressed this lawsuit and asked each party to provide briefs on whether this court case should proceed.<ref>{{cite web |last=Kremer |first=Rich |date=2025-09-25 |title=Wisconsin Supreme Court orders legal briefs in 2 congressional map challenges |url=https://www.wpr.org/news/wisconsin-supreme-court-orders-legal-briefs-in-2-congressional-map-challenges |access-date=2025-10-01 |publisher=WPR |language=en-US}}</ref> On March 31, 2026, a judicial panel dismissed the challenge.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Rego |first1=Max |title=Judicial panel dismisses challenge to Wisconsin congressional map |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5809613-wisconsin-congressional-map-challenge/ |work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] |access-date=April 1, 2026 |date=March 31, 2026}}</ref>
